 60CA    9BE(70ZN,X):NUCID:XUNDL-1     2018TA17                           201808
 60CA c  Compiled (unevaluated) dataset from 2018Ta17:                          
 60CA2c  Phys Rev Lett 121, 022501 (2018).                                      
 60CA c  Compiled by B. Singh (McMaster); August 12, 2018.                      
 60CA c  This work reports discovery of {+60}Ca isotope.                        
 60CA c  2018Ta17: {+60}Ca formed by fragmentation of {+70}Zn{+30+} beam at     
 60CA2c  345 MeV/nucleon from RIKEN-RIBF accelerator complex. Rotating target   
 60CA3c  of {+9}Be of 15 mm thickness were located at the BigRIPS two-stage ion 
 60CA4c  separator. Particle identification (PID) was achieved by measuring     
 60CA5c  time of flight (tof), energy loss (|DE), total kinetic energy (TKE),   
 60CA6c  and magnetic rigidity (B|r) through event by event analysis of         
 60CA7c  reaction products. Particles of interest were stopped in a 76-mm       
 60CA8c  thick CsI crystal after passing through six 1-mm thick silicon p-i-n   
 60CA9c  diodes, while the magnetic rigidity (B|r) of the fragments was         
 60CAAc  reconstructed from position and angle measurements at foci using two   
 60CABc  sets of position-sensitive parallel plate avalanche counters (PPACs).  
 60CACc  Optimization was done using LISE{+++} simulation code. Measured        
 60CADc  production cross section. Comparison of production |s with LISE{++}    
 60CAEc  abrasion-ablation model calculations.                                  
 60CA  L 0            0+                                                        
 60CA cL $Measured cross section=2.1|*10{+-16} b {I15} (2018Ta17) from a        
 60CA2cL total of 2 events assigned to {+60}Ca, with tuned setting of the       
 60CA3cL spectrometer for {+60}Ca.                                              

 60SC    9BE(76GE,X):NUCID:XUNDL-1     2009TA05                           200904
 60SC c  Compiled (unevaluated) dataset from 2009Ta05:                          
 60SC2c  Phys Rev Lett 102, 142501 (2009).                                      
 60SC c  Compiled by A. MacDonald and B. Singh (McMaster); April 9, 2009.       
 60SC c  {+60}Sc formed by fragmentation of {+76}Ge beam at 132 MeV/nucleon     
 60SC2c  at NSCL facility using A1900 fragment separator combined with S800     
 60SC3c  analysis beam line to form a two stage separator system. The           
 60SC4c  transmitted fragments were analyzed event-by-event in momentum and     
 60SC5c  particle identification. The nuclei of interest were stopped in eight  
 60SC6c  Si diodes which provided measurement of energy loss, nuclear charge    
 60SC7c  and total kinetic energy. The time-of-flight of each particle that     
 60SC8c  reached the detector stack was measured in four different ways using   
 60SC9c  plastic scintillators, Si detectors, and parallel-plate avalanche      
 60SCAc  counters. The simultaneous measurement of |DE signals, the magnetic    
 60SCBc  rigidity, total kinetic energy and the time-of-flight (tof) provided   
 60SCCc  unambiguous identification of the atomic number, charge state and mass 
 60SCDc  number.

 60SC    9BE(70ZN,X):XUNDL-3           2025Ze04                           202602

 60SC2c  Phys Rev Lett 135, 232511 (2025)                                       
 60SC c  Compiled by E.A. McCutchan (NNDC,BNL) January 4, 2026                  
 60SC c  {+60}Sc was produced at the RIBF-RIKEN facility                        
 60SC2c  using the {+9}Be({+70}Zn,X) reaction at E=352 MeV/nucleon. Target was  
 60SC3c  8-mm thick {+9}Be. BigRIPS was tuned to optimize transmission of       
 60SC4c  {+55}Ca, which was incident on alternately a carbon target and a       
 60SC5c  target frame. The identification of the nuclide of interest was        
 60SC6c  made in the BigRIPS separator by determining the atomic number and the 
 60SC7c  mass-to-charge ratio of the ion using the tof-B|r-|DE method. The      
 60SC8c  reaction products were transported through the ZeroDegree Spectrometer 
 60SC9c  and implanted into the ZeroDegree Advanced Decay Station (ZD-ADS).     
 60SCac  The ZD-ADS is composed of an implantation detector, LaBr{-3} detectors,
 60SCbc  a neutron tof array and a veto detector. Measured: implanted           
 60SCcc  ion-|b{+-}-t, implanted ion-|b{+-}-|g-t and implanted ions-|g-t        
 60SCdc  correlations.                                                          
 60SCec  Deduced: T{-1/2}.

 60SC2 L %B-=100                                                                
 60SC cL %B-$Only |b{+-} decay mode is expected.

 60SC2cL unbinned maximum-likelihood method. The data analysis                  
 60SC3cL included contributions from the parent, daughter and grand-daughter    
 60SC4cL decays, as well as a constant background. Uncertainty includes both    
 60SC5cL statistical and systematic components.

 60NI3cG M. Scheck which states "Concerning the recoils I corrected them for the
 60NI4cG levels which I had in the Darmstadt spectra. There I had fixed the     
 60NI5cG calibration to the {+11}B lines and needed to recalculate the energies.
 60NI6cG For the lines only seen in the Duke Spectra it shouldn't matter there I
 60NI7cG assumed quite large errors of 4 keV, since I've made an internal       
 60NI8cG calibration to the peaks from Darmstadt which by themselves have       
 60NI9cG already large errors".                                                 
 60NI cG RI$Intensities are branching ratios from |G{-f}/|G values given in     
 60NI2cG 2013Sc20, normalized to 100.                                           
 60NI cG M$Multipolarity are deduced using the gamma-ray polarization and       
 60NI2cG angular-correlation analyses. Angular asymmetry ratios for 90|' and    
 60NI3cG 130|' are plotted in Figure 3 of 2013Sc20, but not tabulated           
 60NI4cG in the paper.                                                          
 60NI cG E(b)$ HI|gS experiment indicates a M1/E1 doublet                       
 60NI cG RI(c)$ From Adopted Gammas for {+60}Ni in ENSDF database               
 60NI cG E(X)$From Adopted Gammas for {+60}Ni in ENSDF database; not observed   
 60NI2cG in the present data                                                    
 60NI cG E(Y)$Contaminated by a single-escape peak                              
 60NI cL $ B(E1)(|^) values here are in e{+2}b, and B(E2)(|^) values in         
 60NI2cL e{+2}b{+2}; 2013Sc20 list these in units of e{+2}fm{+2} and            
 60NI3cL e{+2}fm{+4}, respectively. B(M1)(|^) values are in |m{+2}{-N}
